# Conformal flow and the Riemannian Penrose inequality with minimizing frontiers

Coordinate `EN-117-000-0022-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M2-B-ML-M-0540` | `PM-260.10` | status ACTIVE | original slug `Conformal-flow-and-the-Riemannian-Penrose-inequality-with-minimizing-frontiers-September-27-2026` | date 2026-09-27 | derived from the openai/math preprint Conformal-flow-and-the-Riemannian-Penrose-inequality-with-minimizing-frontiers-September-27-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). Publication status: DRAFT.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.node.v2 -->
```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We give a detailed conformal-flow proof of the numerical Riemannian Penrose inequality in every dimension <i>n</i> ≥ 3 for complete asymptotically flat exteriors with nonnegative scalar curvature and full compact frontiers that are outer minimizing and locally perimeter minimizing. The metric extends smoothly through the possibly singular frontier; neither spin nor frontier connectedness is assumed. The proof follows the conformal-flow approach of Bray, Bray–Lee, and Bi–Zhu.",
```

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We give a detailed conformal-flow proof of the numerical Riemannian Penrose inequality in every dimension <i>n</i> ≥ 3 for complete asymptotically flat exteriors with nonnegative scalar curvature and full compact frontiers that are outer minimizing and locally perimeter minimizing. The metric extends smoothly through the possibly singular frontier; neither spin nor frontier connectedness is assumed. The proof follows the conformal-flow approach of Bray, Bray–Lee, and Bi–Zhu.

- **claim-full-frontier-penrose** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-rpi)): Let $(E,h)$ be a connected complete $n$-dimensional exterior, including its compact frontier $\Sigma$ in the enclosing-set sense above. Suppose that $h$ is smooth on $E$ and through $\Sigma$, and that* 1. *$R_h\ge0$, $R_h\in L^1(E,dV_h)$, and $R_h=O(r^{-n-\beta})$ for some $\beta>0$ on the end;* 2. *outside a compact set there is exactly one Euclidean coordinate end with $h-\delta=O_2(r^{-\tau})$, where $\tau>d/2$;* 3. *the full frontier is outer minimizing and locally perimeter minimizing as the boundary of the filled side. Its regular part is a smooth embedded minimal hypersurface, and its singular set has Hausdorff dimension at most $n-8$.* *Then $$\label{eq:rpi:numerical} m_{\rm ADM}(h)\ge\frac12 \left(\frac{\mathcal H_h^k(\Sigma)}{\omega}\right)^{d/k}.$$
  - Explanation: The ADM mass bounds the full perimeter of the minimizing frontier, including disconnected components and possible frontier singularities. The ambient metric must remain smooth through that frontier.
- **claim-full-enclosure-minimum** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-g-enclosure)): Under the preceding hypotheses, $$0<a_g(B)=\min_{E\in\mathcal A}P_g(E) \le\operatorname{Area}_g(B). \label{eq:g-minimum}$$ All minimizers are supported in one compact subset of $M$. The family of minimizers is compact for $L^1(M)$ convergence, and that convergence also entails convergence of their full perimeters.* *Each minimizer has a closed representative $E$ whose frontier is $T=\operatorname{supp}\|D\mathbf1_E\|_g$. Its exterior $M\setminus E$ is connected and contains the distant end. There are full smooth enclosing cuts in $\operatorname{int}X$ with areas tending to $P_g(E)$. When $3\le n\le7$, these cuts can be chosen to converge to $T$ in $C^1$. Every minimizer is outer minimizing: if $F\supset E$ is bounded and has finite perimeter, then $P_g(F)\ge P_g(E)$.
  - Explanation: The infimum over full smooth enclosing cuts equals an attained positive perimeter minimum. Minimizers have compact support, connected end exteriors, smooth cut recovery, and outer minimization with contact area retained.
- **claim-smooth-obstacle-regularity** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-g-obstacle)): Let $M^n$ carry a smooth metric, let $O$ have smooth boundary, and let $E$ locally minimize perimeter among sets containing $O$. On a relatively compact region meeting $\partial O$, assume that $\partial O$ has bounded smooth geometry. Then $E$ has a closed representative whose topological frontier is $T=\operatorname{supp}\|D\mathbf1_E\|_g$. Its regular part has multiplicity one, and its singular set $\mathcal S$ satisfies $$\mathcal S=\varnothing\quad(n\le7),\qquad \dim_{\mathrm H}\mathcal S\le n-8\quad(n\ge8).$$ The regular part outside $\partial O$ is smooth and minimal. Every contact point in $T\cap\partial O$ is regular, with local graph in $C^{1,\alpha}\cap W^{2,p}$ for every $0<\alpha<1$ and every $1<p<\infty$. In dimensions $3\le n\le7$ the entire frontier is $C^{1,1}$, with local graph functions in $W^{2,\infty}$. Moreover, $\mathcal H^{n-1}_g(\mathcal S)=0$ and $\mathcal H^{n-1}_g(T)=P_g(E)$ wherever the total perimeter is finite.
  - Explanation: Smooth obstacle contact is regular even when the free frontier can be singular. The result distinguishes regularity at contact from smooth minimality away from the obstacle and identifies full perimeter with frontier area.
- **claim-strict-barrier-uniform-detachment** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-g-detachment)): In the setting of Lemma <a href="#lem:g-enclosure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:g-enclosure">2</a>, suppose that $H_B=\operatorname{div}_B\nu_B<0$ on every component of $B$, with $\nu_B$ pointing into $X$. There is $\varepsilon>0$, depending only on a fixed collar of $(B,g)$, such that every minimizing filled set contains the exterior distance collar $0\le t<\varepsilon$ in its interior. Consequently its frontier $T$ is compact, nonempty, and disjoint from $B$, and is locally perimeter minimizing in the smooth ambient metric. It is stationary as a whole boundary varifold, smooth and minimal outside a compact singular set of dimension at most $n-8$. If $3\le n\le7$, $T$ is a smooth minimal full cut.* *The closed exterior $D=\overline{M\setminus E}$ is connected, has the same single coordinate end, and is a complete closed subset for the ambient distance. Every sequence that is Cauchy for lengths of paths constrained to $D$ has a limit in $D$ in the same sense, including when the limit lies on the frontier. Its frontier is outer minimizing with all components counted, and $\mathcal H^{n-1}_g(T)=a_g(B)$.
  - Explanation: Strictly negative mean curvature toward the end forces every minimizing enclosure to fill a common exterior collar. Its detached frontier is locally minimizing, and its closed exterior is complete with full perimeter counted.
- **claim-strict-inner-barrier-penrose** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-g-strict-barrier-penrose)): Let $(X,g)$ be a smooth connected $n$-dimensional exterior, $n\ge3$, complete including its nonempty compact smooth boundary $B$, with one Euclidean coordinate end and compact end complement. Suppose that $$g-\delta=O_2(r^{-\tau}),\qquad \tau>\frac{n-2}{2},\qquad R_g\ge0,\quad R_g\in L^1(X,dV_g),\quad R_g=O(r^{-n-\beta})\quad(\beta>0).$$ If $H_B<0$ for the normal pointing into $X$, then $$m_{\rm ADM}(g)\ge\frac12 \left(\frac{a_g(B)}{\omega}\right)^{(n-2)/(n-1)}.$$
  - Explanation: A smooth strict inner barrier has a mass bound determined by its full enclosing area, even when the original boundary does not itself minimize perimeter.
- **claim-area-preserving-conformal-flow** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-rpi-flow-construction)): Let $K_0$ be the initial outermost minimizing enclosure just described, and put $$E_0=M\setminus K_0,\qquad \Sigma_0=\partial K_0, \qquad A_0=P_{g_0}(K_0).$$ Here and below perimeter is relative to $\operatorname{int}M$ and counts the whole frontier. There are increasing closed filled sets $K_t\supset K_0$, with connected end exteriors $E_t=M\setminus K_t$, and positive continuous functions $u_t$ such that $$\label{eq:rpi:flow-integral} \begin{gathered} g_t=u_t^{4/d}g_0,\qquad u_t=1+\int_0^t v_s\,ds,\\ \Delta_{g_0}v_t=0\quad\hbox{in }E_t,\qquad v_t=0\quad\hbox{on }K_t,\qquad v_t\longrightarrow-e^{-t}\quad\hbox{at infinity}. \end{gathered}$$ For every $t$ we have $e^{-t}\le u_t\le1$. Each $K_t$ locally minimizes perimeter in the continuous metric $g_t$ against all variations in balls below the initial comparison radius, including variations across $K_0$. The set $K_t$ is the outermost $g_t$-minimizing enclosure of $K_0$ and satisfies $$\label{eq:rpi:flow-area} P_{g_t}(K_t)=A_0.$$ On each finite time interval the conductors have common compact support, the factors have a common positive lower bound and spatial Hölder modulus, and the frontiers have uniform two-phase density and almost-minimizing estimates. In particular there are $\alpha_T>0$, $C_T<\infty$, and $r_T>0$ such that $$\label{eq:rpi:flow-almost-minimizing} P_{g_0}(K_t;B_r(x)) \le P_{g_0}(L;B_r(x))+C_T r^{k+\alpha_T}$$ whenever $0\le t\le T$, $r<r_T$, and $L\mathbin{\triangle}K_t\Subset B_r(x)$.
  - Explanation: The conformal flow couples harmonic velocities to increasing outermost minimizing conductors and preserves the full frontier area. Uniform finite-time support, density, Hölder continuity, and almost-minimizing bounds control singular frontiers.
- **claim-weighted-hull-escape-potential** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-rpi-static-hull-potential)): Every compact finite-perimeter obstacle has a unique outermost minimizing enclosure for any continuous perimeter weight bounded above and below by positive constants.* *For the quantitative conclusions, let $K_0$ be a compact finite-perimeter filled set containing a collar of $B$, locally perimeter minimizing in $g_0$ with comparison radius $r_0>0$. Put $A_0=P_{g_0}(K_0)$, and let $a$ be continuous with $c\le a\le1$ and $a=1$ on $K_0$, where $c>0$. Every $P_a$-minimizing enclosure $K$ has weighted perimeter at most $A_0$ and is contained in a ball $B_{R_*(c)}$ depending only on $c$, $A_0$, the initial support, and the reference geometry.* *Every such enclosure locally minimizes $P_a$ against unconstrained variations in balls of radius less than $r_0$. Its canonical closed representative has connected open end exterior and uniform two-phase volume and perimeter density bounds. The unique escape potential $q_K$, harmonic in that exterior, zero on $K$, and tending to one at infinity, is continuous and has a Hölder modulus depending on the same data. It satisfies $0<q_K<1$ in the exterior.* *If in addition $\operatorname{osc}_{B_r}a\le C_a r^\alpha$ for $0<\alpha<1$, then, for local competitors $L$, $$P_{g_0}(K;B_r)\le P_{g_0}(L;B_r)+C r^{k+\alpha},$$ where $C$ also depends on $C_a$.
  - Explanation: A positive continuous perimeter weight gives a unique outermost hull. Initial local minimality and a weight fixed on the initial obstacle provide unconstrained local comparison, compact confinement, density bounds, and a continuous escape potential; Hölder weights also give an additive perimeter error.
- **claim-discrete-flow-uniform-estimates** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-rpi-discrete-flow)): For every finite $T$, the preceding induction defines the flow on $[0,T]$. Every grid conductor $K_j^\epsilon$ is the outermost $h_j^\epsilon$-minimizing enclosure of the fixed $K_0$. The conductors are nested and have common compact support. Their velocities, and the factors $u_t^\epsilon$, have a common spatial Hölder modulus; $u_t^\epsilon$ is also uniformly Lipschitz in time. The frontiers have uniform density and background almost-minimizing estimates. Their grid areas $A_j^\epsilon=P_{h_j^\epsilon}(K_j^\epsilon)$ are nonincreasing, and the area is frozen between grid times.
  - Explanation: The discrete induction keeps each grid conductor outermost for the fixed initial obstacle. Nested conductors, uniform spatial and temporal control, and frozen between-grid areas prepare the continuum limit.
- **claim-vanishing-discrete-area-loss** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-rpi-discrete-area)): For every finite $T$, the areas of the discrete frontiers tend uniformly to $A_0$ for $0\le t\le T$ as $\epsilon\downarrow0$.
  - Explanation: Discrete frontier areas converge uniformly to the initial area on each finite time interval. This establishes area preservation before nesting of the limiting outermost hulls is derived.
- **claim-simultaneous-regular-neighborhoods** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-rpi-simultaneous-regularity)): Fix a regular point $p$ of one $\Sigma_t$ and a coordinate plane parallel to its tangent plane there. Given a sufficiently small slope bound, there are nested coordinate neighborhoods $U\Subset V$ of $p$ such that every frontier meeting $U$ is a single graph in $V$ over that plane, with the prescribed slope bound and a uniform $C^{1,\theta}$ estimate. On smaller neighborhoods, every fixed finite order of one-sided regularity of the graphs, harmonic velocities, and conformal factors is uniform for $0\le t\le T$. These conclusions also hold uniformly for the rescaled time families on each fixed compact regular patch of a limiting cone.
  - Explanation: A regular point supplies common graphical neighborhoods for all flow frontiers meeting a smaller region. One-sided harmonic estimates and the graph equation then give uniform fixed-order regularity, including on regular patches of blow-up cones.
- **claim-relative-harmonic-derivative-control** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-rpi-relative-harmonic)): Consider uniformly $C^{2,\alpha}$ graphical domains in a fixed cylinder, with uniformly elliptic $C^{2,\alpha}$ metric coefficients. Let $z_i>0$ be harmonic on the exterior side, with zero boundary trace, and let $A_i$ be interior points whose distances from the graph and the lateral boundary are bounded below by a fixed positive fraction of the cylinder radius. These are the interior corkscrew points used in the estimate. Put $a_i=z_i(A_i)$. On nested central cylinders, $$\\|z_i\\|_{C^{2,\alpha}}\le C a_i, \qquad c a_i\le\partial_\nu z_i\le C a_i$$ on the original boundary, where $\nu$ points into the exterior. If a second graph lies in the closure of that exterior and converges in $C^1$ to the first, then on the second graph the derivative in its corresponding exterior-pointing unit normal also lies between $c a_i$ and $C a_i$, with adjusted fixed constants, for large $i$. All assertions are relative to $a_i$; no positive lower bound for $a_i$ is required.
  - Explanation: Harmonic values at controlled interior points normalize boundary derivatives and higher regularity. Derivatives transferred to nearby graphs remain comparable to that normalization even when it tends to zero.
- **claim-singular-support-separation** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-rpi-singular-separation)): Let $K\subset K'$ be canonical closed filled sets in a smooth $n$-manifold, $n\ge8$, with multiplicity-one perimeter supports $\Sigma$ and $\Sigma'$. Put $k=n-1$. On a neighborhood of their possible contact set, let $g_{\rm ref}$ be a smooth metric and assume that there are $\Lambda<\infty$, $\alpha>0$, and $r_0>0$ such that, for $E=K,K'$, $$P_{g_{\rm ref}}(E;B_r(x)) \le P_{g_{\rm ref}}(L;B_r(x))+\Lambda r^{k+\alpha} \quad\text{if }L\triangle E\Subset B_r(x),\quad 0<r<r_0.$$ The balls lie in that neighborhood. Assume that every intersection point is singular for both supports.* *Let $g=a^{4/(n-2)}g_{\rm ref}$, with $a>0$ continuous, be the metric used to measure graph heights. At a possible contact $p$, choose coordinates with $g(p)=\delta$ and rescale by $\eta_i(y)=p+r_i y$, $r_i\downarrow0$. The power comparison gives simultaneous minimizing-cone subsequences with local perimeter convergence; the two limiting cones coincide, as shown below. For every such subsequence with common cone $C$, assume the following on each compact regular patch, after shrinking a larger regular patch:* 1. *The rescaled supports converge as single smooth graphs, and extensions of $g_i=r_i^{-2}\eta_i^*g$ converge to $\delta$ in $C^2$. The graph and metric derivatives needed below have uniform local bounds.* 2. *The later support is the $g_i$-normal graph of a function $\zeta_i>0$ over the earlier support, and $$\label{eq:rpi:separation-interface} L_i\zeta_i=F_i\le0,\qquad L_i\longrightarrow J_C:=\Delta_C+\|A_C\|^2.$$ In fixed regular-patch coordinates, the second-order operators are uniformly elliptic, their coefficients have uniform $C^{0,\theta}$ bounds for some $\theta>0$, and converge locally to those of $J_C$.* 3. *On a slightly larger patch there are numbers $\lambda_i>0$ and constants $c,C>0$, independent of $i$, such that $$-C\lambda_i\le F_i\le0,\qquad F_i\le-c\lambda_i\quad\text{on an inner ball}.$$ The numbers and constants may depend on the fixed patch, and $\lambda_i$ may tend to zero.* *Then $\Sigma\cap\Sigma'=\varnothing$.
  - Explanation: Ordered supports with no regular contact are disjoint when every common-cone blow-up satisfies the specified smooth graph, elliptic operator, and relative forcing hypotheses. Absence of regular contact alone is insufficient.
- **claim-flow-and-perturbed-enclosure-detachment** (construction, [source](#stmt-cor-rpi-flow-detachment)): For any fixed $0\le s<t$, the conformal-flow frontiers $\Sigma_s$ and $\Sigma_t$ are disjoint as closed perimeter supports. For either the flow or smooth initial exterior in Section <a href="#sec:rpi:perturbed-candidate" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:rpi:perturbed-candidate">6.3</a>, let $u$ satisfy the harmonic, boundary, strictness, and end hypotheses stated there. For the forcing and sufficiently small fixed $\delta>0$ constructed there, the perimeter-plus-volume minimizer is disjoint from the original frontier, including its singular set. Each fixed pair of these compact supports has positive distance.
  - Explanation: Distinct fixed-time flow supports are disjoint, including at singular points. A strictly decreasing harmonic conformal perturbation also detaches its prescribed-curvature minimizing enclosure; compactness gives positive distance for each fixed pair.
- **claim-one-sided-curvature-preserving-smoothing** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-rpi-one-sided-smoothing)): Let $\Omega$ be an open subset of a smooth Riemannian manifold with compact full boundary $\Sigma$. Assume that the metric is smooth on an ambient neighborhood of $\Sigma$. Let $\Lambda\subset\Sigma$ consist of the points touched by a geodesic ball contained in $\Omega$. Suppose that $\Lambda$ is a smooth embedded hypersurface, locally bounds $\Omega$ on its accessible side, and has mean curvature at least $\gamma>0$ for the normal pointing out of $\Omega$.* *For every $\varepsilon>0$, there is an open set $\Omega_\varepsilon\subset\Omega$ with smooth compact boundary such that $$\Omega\setminus\Omega_\varepsilon \subset\{x\in\Omega:\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(x,\Sigma)<\varepsilon\}, \qquad H_{\mathrm{out},\Omega_\varepsilon}\ge\gamma-\varepsilon.$$
  - Explanation: Accessible boundary points with a positive outward curvature bound permit smooth interior approximations in arbitrarily small collars. The conclusion controls inclusion and curvature, without asserting boundary-area convergence.
- **claim-nonnegative-mass-harmonic-ends** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-rpi-harmonic-pmt)): A smooth complete boundaryless manifold of dimension $n\ge8$ with finitely many ends, all harmonically flat, and nonnegative scalar curvature has nonnegative ADM mass at each end.
  - Explanation: Complete smooth boundaryless manifolds with finitely many harmonically flat ends have nonnegative mass at each end under nonnegative scalar curvature. The proof reduces this statement to an independent smooth positive-mass theorem.
- **claim-smooth-mass-capacity-inequality** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-rpi-mass-capacity)): Let $(N,h)$, of dimension $n\ge8$, be a smooth connected exterior, complete including its compact smooth boundary, with exactly one end and compact end complement. Suppose the end is harmonically flat and the scalar curvature is nonnegative. Write $\Gamma$ for its boundary. If $H_\nu\le0$ for the normal toward the end, then $m_{\rm ADM}(h)\ge\mathfrak c(\Gamma,h)$.
  - Explanation: For a complete smooth harmonic-end exterior, nonpositive mean curvature toward the end makes boundary capacity a lower bound for ADM mass. Positive conformal doubling factors keep both ends complete during the positive-mass comparison.
- **claim-flow-mass-capacity-evolution** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-rpi-mass-evolution)): For the conformal flow of Lemma <a href="#lem:rpi:flow-construction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:rpi:flow-construction">6</a> in dimension $n\ge8$, with nonempty initial frontier and harmonically flat initial end, let $m(t)$ be its ADM mass in normalized coordinates and let $c_t=\mathfrak c(\Sigma_t,g_t)$. Then $$m(t)\ge c_t\ge0,\qquad \mu(t):=m(t)-c_t\ge0.$$ The mass is locally absolutely continuous and satisfies $m'(t)=-2\mu(t)$ almost everywhere.
  - Explanation: Mass dominates capacity on every slice of the harmonic-end flow. Their nonnegative difference drives the almost-everywhere mass derivative, so the locally absolutely continuous mass is nonincreasing.
- **claim-deficit-decay-normalized-confinement** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-rpi-flow-estimates)): For the flow in Proposition <a href="#prop:rpi:mass-evolution" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:rpi:mass-evolution">18</a>, $\mu(t)\to0$ as $t\to\infty$. There is a fixed $R_{\max}$ such that $$\label{eq:rpi:radius-bound} \Sigma_t\subset B_{R_{\max}e^{2t/d}}\qquad(t\ge0).$$ Here the radius is measured in the original end coordinates, with the compact core included in the ball. Equivalently, every normalized frontier lies in $B_{R_{\max}}$.
  - Explanation: The mass–capacity deficit tends to zero, and normalized frontiers remain in a fixed ball. The radius in original coordinates grows with the coordinate normalization.
- **claim-complete-auxiliary-factor-approximants** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-rpi-complete-end-factors)): For the normalized flow of Proposition <a href="#prop:rpi:mass-evolution" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:rpi:mass-evolution">18</a> there are fixed radii $R_c<R_1$ and complete smooth connected boundaryless one-ended metrics with nonnegative scalar curvature, harmonic end factors $\mathcal Z_t$ on $\{r>R_1\}$, and masses $\mathcal M_t$, such that $$0\le\mathcal M_t\longrightarrow0,\qquad \sup_{r\ge2R_1}r^d\|\mathcal Z_t-J_t\|\longrightarrow0.$$ The radius $R_c$ encloses every normalized frontier and compact core. For every fixed $s>R_c$, $$\label{eq:rpi:auxiliary-weighted-limit} \sup_{r\ge s}r^d\|J_t-1\|\longrightarrow0.$$
  - Explanation: The auxiliary harmonic end factor is approximated by complete smooth connected boundaryless one-ended metrics with nonnegative curvature and masses tending to zero. Quantitative positive mass then yields weighted convergence on every fixed tail beyond the enclosing radius.
- **claim-quantitative-one-harmonic-end** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-rpi-quantitative-one-end)): For each $n\ge8$ there is a fixed constant $\ell_n>1$ such that, for every $\eta>0$, there is $\delta(n,\eta)>0$ with the following property. Let $(N,h)$ be a smooth complete connected boundaryless $n$-manifold with $R_h\ge0$. Suppose that outside a compact set it consists of a single coordinate end $\{r>R\}$, where $R>0$, and that on this end $$h=Z^{4/(n-2)}\delta,\qquad Z>0,\qquad \Delta_\delta Z=0,\qquad Z\longrightarrow1.$$ Writing $d=n-2$ and $m=m_{\rm ADM}(h)$, we have $$m<\delta(n,\eta)R^d \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \sup_{r>\ell_nR}\|Z-1\|<\eta.$$
  - Explanation: A sufficiently small mass relative to the harmonic coordinate scale forces the positive harmonic end factor to be uniformly close to its asymptotic constant beyond a dimensional enlargement. The compact interior may vary, but completeness and the single-end condition remain essential.
- **claim-harmonic-flat-full-area-penrose** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-rpi-harmonic-flat-inequality)): Let $(E,h)$ satisfy the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:rpi" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:rpi">1</a>, with $n\ge8$, nonempty frontier of full area $A$, and a harmonically flat end. Then $$m_{\rm ADM}(h)\ge\frac12(A/\omega)^{d/(n-1)}.$$
  - Explanation: The harmonic-end conformal flow proves the sharp mass bound for a nonempty minimizing frontier. Its proof uses the largest limiting frontier radius and enclosing spheres rather than convergence of every component.
- **claim-smooth-harmonic-flat-density** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-rpi-smooth-density)): Let $(N,h)$ be a smooth connected asymptotically flat exterior, complete including its compact smooth boundary $\Gamma$, possibly empty, with one coordinate end and compact end complement. Suppose that $$h-\delta=O_2(r^{-\tau}),\qquad \tau>d/2,\qquad R_h\ge0,\quad R_h\in L^1,\quad R_h=O(r^{-n-\beta})$$ for some $\beta>0$, and that its ADM mass exists. There are smooth metrics $\widehat h_R$, complete with the same boundary, with nonnegative scalar curvature and a harmonically flat end, such that $$\begin{gathered} (1-\epsilon_R)h\le\widehat h_R\le(1+\epsilon_R)h,\qquad \epsilon_R\longrightarrow0,\\ \widehat h_R\longrightarrow h\ \text{in }C^2 \text{ on every fixed compact set},\\ m_{\rm ADM}(\widehat h_R)\longrightarrow m_{\rm ADM}(h). \end{gathered}$$ In particular, if $H_\nu(\Gamma,h)<0$ for the normal toward the end, the same strict sign holds for $\widehat h_R$ when $R$ is large.
  - Explanation: A distant cutoff and Dirichlet conformal correction approximate a smooth exterior by harmonic-end metrics with nonnegative scalar curvature. Global metric comparison, compact regular convergence, and mass convergence allow passage of enclosing-area inequalities; strict boundary curvature persists.
- **claim-weighted-minimizer-singular-tube** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-rpi-bw-quantitative)): Let $n\ge8$, let $g$ be smooth on an open set $U$, and let $\widehat\rho>0$ and $\Phi$ be smooth there. Put $$g_* =\widehat\rho^{2/(n-1)}g, \qquad f=\Phi\widehat\rho^{-1/(n-1)}.$$ Suppose that a locally finite-perimeter set $E$ locally minimizes $$\mathcal F(E;W) =P_{g_*}(E;W)-\int_{E\cap W}f\,dV_{g_*}$$ against changes compactly supported in $W\Subset U$. Let $\mathcal S$ be a compact subset of the singular set of its perimeter support in $U$. There are constants $C,t_0>0$ such that $$\label{eq:rpi:bw-tube} \mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}_g\bigl(B_t^g(\mathcal S)\bigr)\le C t^8, \qquad 0<t<t_0.$$ The constants are local: they may depend on the minimizer and on a buffered compact neighborhood of $\mathcal S$.
  - Explanation: Local minimization of a positive weighted perimeter with smooth volume forcing gives a tube-volume bound for compact singular subsets. The constants depend on the minimizer and buffered local geometry; bounded mean curvature alone does not provide the required symmetry exclusion.

Suppose that $$g-\\delta=O_2(r^{-\\tau}),\\qquad \\tau>\\frac{n-2}{2},\\qquad\n R_g\\ge0,\\quad R_g\\in L^1(X,dV_g),\\quad\n R_g=O(r^{-n-\\beta})\\quad(\\beta>0).$$ If $H_B<0$ for the normal pointing into $X$, then $$m_{\\rm ADM}(g)\\ge\\frac12\n \\left(\\frac{a_g(B)}{\\omega}\\right)^{(n-2)/(n-1)}.$$",
```json
          "source_id": "PM-260.10"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-rpi-flow-construction",
        "exact_quote": "Let $K_0$ be the initial outermost minimizing enclosure just described, and put $$E_0=M\\setminus K_0,\\qquad \\Sigma_0=\\partial K_0,\n \\qquad A_0=P_{g_0}(K_0).$$ Here and below perimeter is relative to $\\operatorname{int}M$ and counts the whole frontier. There are increasing closed filled sets $K_t\\supset K_0$, with connected end exteriors $E_t=M\\setminus K_t$, and positive continuous functions $u_t$ such that $$\\label{eq:rpi:flow-integral}\n \\begin{gathered}\n g_t=u_t^{4/d}g_0,\\qquad\n u_t=1+\\int_0^t v_s\\,ds,\\\\\n \\Delta_{g_0}v_t=0\\quad\\hbox{in }E_t,\\qquad\n v_t=0\\quad\\hbox{on }K_t,\\qquad\n v_t\\longrightarrow-e^{-t}\\quad\\hbox{at infinity}.\n \\end{gathered}$$ For every $t$ we have $e^{-t}\\le u_t\\le1$. Each $K_t$ locally minimizes perimeter in the continuous metric $g_t$ against all variations in balls below the initial comparison radius, including variations across $K_0$. The set $K_t$ is the outermost $g_t$-minimizing enclosure of $K_0$ and satisfies $$\\label{eq:rpi:flow-area}\n P_{g_t}(K_t)=A_0.$$ On each finite time interval the conductors have common compact support, the factors have a common positive lower bound and spatial Hölder modulus, and the frontiers have uniform two-phase density and almost-minimizing estimates. In particular there are $\\alpha_T>0$, $C_T<\\infty$, and $r_T>0$ such that $$\\label{eq:rpi:flow-almost-minimizing}\n P_{g_0}(K_t;B_r(x))\n \\le P_{g_0}(L;B_r(x))+C_T r^{k+\\alpha_T}$$ whenever $0\\le t\\le T$, $r<r_T$, and $L\\mathbin{\\triangle}K_t\\Subset B_r(x)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.10"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-rpi-static-hull-potential",
        "exact_quote": "Every compact finite-perimeter obstacle has a unique outermost minimizing enclosure for any continuous perimeter weight bounded above and below by positive constants.*\n\n*For the quantitative conclusions, let $K_0$ be a compact finite-perimeter filled set containing a collar of $B$, locally perimeter minimizing in $g_0$ with comparison radius $r_0>0$. Put $A_0=P_{g_0}(K_0)$, and let $a$ be continuous with $c\\le a\\le1$ and $a=1$ on $K_0$, where $c>0$. Every $P_a$-minimizing enclosure $K$ has weighted perimeter at most $A_0$ and is contained in a ball $B_{R_*(c)}$ depending only on $c$, $A_0$, the initial support, and the reference geometry.*\n\n*Every such enclosure locally minimizes $P_a$ against unconstrained variations in balls of radius less than $r_0$. Its canonical closed representative has connected open end exterior and uniform two-phase volume and perimeter density bounds. The unique escape potential $q_K$, harmonic in that exterior, zero on $K$, and tending to one at infinity, is continuous and has a Hölder modulus depending on the same data. It satisfies $0<q_K<1$ in the exterior.*\n\n*If in addition $\\operatorname{osc}_{B_r}a\\le C_a r^\\alpha$ for $0<\\alpha<1$, then, for local competitors $L$, $$P_{g_0}(K;B_r)\\le P_{g_0}(L;B_r)+C r^{k+\\alpha},$$ where $C$ also depends on $C_a$.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.10"
      },

        {

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-rpi-discrete-flow",
        "exact_quote": "For every finite $T$, the preceding induction defines the flow on $[0,T]$. Every grid conductor $K_j^\\epsilon$ is the outermost $h_j^\\epsilon$-minimizing enclosure of the fixed $K_0$. The conductors are nested and have common compact support. Their velocities, and the factors $u_t^\\epsilon$, have a common spatial Hölder modulus; $u_t^\\epsilon$ is also uniformly Lipschitz in time. The frontiers have uniform density and background almost-minimizing estimates. Their grid areas $A_j^\\epsilon=P_{h_j^\\epsilon}(K_j^\\epsilon)$ are nonincreasing, and the area is frozen between grid times.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.10"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-rpi-simultaneous-regularity",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a regular point $p$ of one $\\Sigma_t$ and a coordinate plane parallel to its tangent plane there. Given a sufficiently small slope bound, there are nested coordinate neighborhoods $U\\Subset V$ of $p$ such that every frontier meeting $U$ is a single graph in $V$ over that plane, with the prescribed slope bound and a uniform $C^{1,\\theta}$ estimate. On smaller neighborhoods, every fixed finite order of one-sided regularity of the graphs, harmonic velocities, and conformal factors is uniform for $0\\le t\\le T$. These conclusions also hold uniformly for the rescaled time families on each fixed compact regular patch of a limiting cone.",

      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-rpi-relative-harmonic",
        "exact_quote": "Consider uniformly $C^{2,\\alpha}$ graphical domains in a fixed cylinder, with uniformly elliptic $C^{2,\\alpha}$ metric coefficients. Let $z_i>0$ be harmonic on the exterior side, with zero boundary trace, and let $A_i$ be interior points whose distances from the graph and the lateral boundary are bounded below by a fixed positive fraction of the cylinder radius. These are the interior corkscrew points used in the estimate. Put $a_i=z_i(A_i)$. On nested central cylinders, $$\\|z_i\\|_{C^{2,\\alpha}}\\le C a_i,\n \\qquad c a_i\\le\\partial_\\nu z_i\\le C a_i$$ on the original boundary, where $\\nu$ points into the exterior. If a second graph lies in the closure of that exterior and converges in $C^1$ to the first, then on the second graph the derivative in its corresponding exterior-pointing unit normal also lies between $c a_i$ and $C a_i$, with adjusted fixed constants, for large $i$. All assertions are relative to $a_i$; no positive lower bound for $a_i$ is required.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.10"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-rpi-relative-harmonic",
          "exact_quote": "Consider uniformly $C^{2,\\alpha}$ graphical domains in a fixed cylinder, with uniformly elliptic $C^{2,\\alpha}$ metric coefficients. Let $z_i>0$ be harmonic on the exterior side, with zero boundary trace, and let $A_i$ be interior points whose distances from the graph and the lateral boundary are bounded below by a fixed positive fraction of the cylinder radius. These are the interior corkscrew points used in the estimate. Put $a_i=z_i(A_i)$. On nested central cylinders, $$\\|z_i\\|_{C^{2,\\alpha}}\\le C a_i,\n \\qquad c a_i\\le\\partial_\\nu z_i\\le C a_i$$ on the original boundary, where $\\nu$ points into the exterior. If a second graph lies in the closure of that exterior and converges in $C^1$ to the first, then on the second graph the derivative in its corresponding exterior-pointing unit normal also lies between $c a_i$ and $C a_i$, with adjusted fixed constants, for large $i$. All assertions are relative to $a_i$; no positive lower bound for $a_i$ is required.",
          "source_id": "PM-260.10"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Consider uniformly $C^{2,\\alpha}$ graphical domains in a fixed cylinder, with uniformly elliptic $C^{2,\\alpha}$ metric coefficients. All assertions are relative to $a_i$; no positive lower bound for $a_i$ is required.",

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-rpi-singular-separation",
          "exact_quote": "Let $K\\subset K'$ be canonical closed filled sets in a smooth $n$-manifold, $n\\ge8$, with multiplicity-one perimeter supports $\\Sigma$ and $\\Sigma'$. Put $k=n-1$. On a neighborhood of their possible contact set, let $g_{\\rm ref}$ be a smooth metric and assume that there are $\\Lambda<\\infty$, $\\alpha>0$, and $r_0>0$ such that, for $E=K,K'$, $$P_{g_{\\rm ref}}(E;B_r(x))\n \\le P_{g_{\\rm ref}}(L;B_r(x))+\\Lambda r^{k+\\alpha}\n \\quad\\text{if }L\\triangle E\\Subset B_r(x),\\quad 0<r<r_0.$$ The balls lie in that neighborhood. Assume that every intersection point is singular for both supports.*\n\n*Let $g=a^{4/(n-2)}g_{\\rm ref}$, with $a>0$ continuous, be the metric used to measure graph heights. At a possible contact $p$, choose coordinates with $g(p)=\\delta$ and rescale by $\\eta_i(y)=p+r_i y$, $r_i\\downarrow0$. The power comparison gives simultaneous minimizing-cone subsequences with local perimeter convergence; the two limiting cones coincide, as shown below. For every such subsequence with common cone $C$, assume the following on each compact regular patch, after shrinking a larger regular patch:*\n\n1.  *The rescaled supports converge as single smooth graphs, and extensions of $g_i=r_i^{-2}\\eta_i^*g$ converge to $\\delta$ in $C^2$. The graph and metric derivatives needed below have uniform local bounds.*\n\n\n2.  *The later support is the $g_i$-normal graph of a function $\\zeta_i>0$ over the earlier support, and $$\\label{eq:rpi:separation-interface}\n     L_i\\zeta_i=F_i\\le0,\\qquad\n     L_i\\longrightarrow J_C:=\\Delta_C+|A_C|^2.$$ In fixed regular-patch coordinates, the second-order operators are uniformly elliptic, their coefficients have uniform $C^{0,\\theta}$ bounds for some $\\theta>0$, and converge locally to those of $J_C$.*\n\n3.  *On a slightly larger patch there are numbers $\\lambda_i>0$ and constants $c,C>0$, independent of $i$, such that $$-C\\lambda_i\\le F_i\\le0,\\qquad\n     F_i\\le-c\\lambda_i\\quad\\text{on an inner ball}.$$ The numbers and constants may depend on the fixed patch, and $\\lambda_i$ may tend to zero.*\n\n*Then $\\Sigma\\cap\\Sigma'=\\varnothing$.",
          "source_id": "PM-260.10"
        }

        "exact_quote": "For any fixed $0\\le s<t$, the conformal-flow frontiers $\\Sigma_s$ and $\\Sigma_t$ are disjoint as closed perimeter supports.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-rpi-one-sided-smoothing",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\Omega$ be an open subset of a smooth Riemannian manifold with compact full boundary $\\Sigma$. Assume that the metric is smooth on an ambient neighborhood of $\\Sigma$. Let $\\Lambda\\subset\\Sigma$ consist of the points touched by a geodesic ball contained in $\\Omega$. Suppose that $\\Lambda$ is a smooth embedded hypersurface, locally bounds $\\Omega$ on its accessible side, and has mean curvature at least $\\gamma>0$ for the normal pointing out of $\\Omega$.*\n\n*For every $\\varepsilon>0$, there is an open set $\\Omega_\\varepsilon\\subset\\Omega$ with smooth compact boundary such that $$\\Omega\\setminus\\Omega_\\varepsilon\n \\subset\\{x\\in\\Omega:\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(x,\\Sigma)<\\varepsilon\\},\n \\qquad\n H_{\\mathrm{out},\\Omega_\\varepsilon}\\ge\\gamma-\\varepsilon.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-260.10"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-rpi-one-sided-smoothing",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\Omega$ be an open subset of a smooth Riemannian manifold with compact full boundary $\\Sigma$. Suppose that $\\Lambda$ is a smooth embedded hypersurface, locally bounds $\\Omega$ on its accessible side, and has mean curvature at least $\\gamma>0$ for the normal pointing out of $\\Omega$.*\n\n*For every $\\varepsilon>0$, there is an open set $\\Omega_\\varepsilon\\subset\\Omega$ with smooth compact boundary such that $$\\Omega\\setminus\\Omega_\\varepsilon\n \\subset\\{x\\in\\Omega:\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(x,\\Sigma)<\\varepsilon\\},\n \\qquad\n H_{\\mathrm{out},\\Omega_\\varepsilon}\\ge\\gamma-\\varepsilon.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-260.10"
        }
      ],
```

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **dimension parameters and sphere area** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Write $n\ge3$, $k=n-1$, $d=n-2$, and $\omega=\mathcal H^k_\delta(S^k)$. We use $O_j(r^{-\gamma})$ for a tensor whose coordinate derivatives of order $\ell\le j$ are $O(r^{-\gamma-\ell})$. On a Euclidean coordinate end the ADM mass is $$m_{\rm ADM}(h)=\frac1{2k\omega} \lim_{R\to\infty}\int_{S_R} (\partial_jh_{ij}-\partial_ih_{jj})n_\delta^i\,dA_\delta.$$ The decay and scalar integrability hypotheses below give the usual well-defined mass in this normalization; see (Bartnik 1986) [[Bartnik1986](#ref-Bartnik1986)].
- **differentiated coordinate decay** ([source](#pm-h-3)): We use $O_j(r^{-\gamma})$ for a tensor whose coordinate derivatives of order $\ell\le j$ are $O(r^{-\gamma-\ell})$.
- **ADM mass** ([source](#pm-h-3)): On a Euclidean coordinate end the ADM mass is $$m_{\rm ADM}(h)=\frac1{2k\omega} \lim_{R\to\infty}\int_{S_R} (\partial_jh_{ij}-\partial_ih_{jj})n_\delta^i\,dA_\delta.$$
- **full frontier area** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Its area is the full reduced-boundary perimeter, including every component.
- **restricted length completeness** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Completeness means that each finite-distance equivalence class $\{q\in E:d_E(p,q)<\infty\}$ is complete for the restricted metric.
- **outer minimizing frontier** ([source](#pm-h-3)): The frontier is *outer minimizing* if every finite-perimeter enclosure of its filled side with compact additional support has at least its perimeter.
- **locally perimeter minimizing frontier** ([source](#pm-h-3)): It is *locally perimeter minimizing* if compactly supported changes in sufficiently small neighborhoods, including changes on either side, cannot reduce perimeter.
- **end-oriented mean curvature** ([source](#pm-h-5)): ###### Mean-curvature convention.
- **full smooth enclosing cut** ([source](#eq:g-full-cut-area)): A *full smooth enclosing cut* is the entire intrinsic manifold boundary $\partial_{\mathrm{man}}D$ of a connected smooth domain $D\subset X$ that is closed in $X$, contains the distant coordinate end, and has compact boundary.
- **full enclosing area** ([source](#eq:g-full-cut-area)): Define $$a_g(B)=\inf_D\operatorname{Area}_g(\partial_{\mathrm{man}}D). \label{eq:g-full-cut-area}$$
- **weighted perimeter** ([source](#pm-h-12)): For a continuous positive weight $a$, write $$P_a(L;U)=\int_{U\cap\partial^*L}a\,d\mathcal H^k_{g_0}.$$
- **homogeneous energy space** ([source](#pm-h-15)): For an individual compact conductor $K$, let $\mathcal D^{1,2}(M)$ be the completion, in the gradient norm, of smooth functions compactly supported relative to $M$.
- **quasi-everywhere** ([source](#pm-h-15)): Here quasi-everywhere means outside a set of Newtonian capacity zero.
- **normalized conductor capacity** ([source](#eq:rpi:capacity)): For a smooth compact conductor with exterior $(E,h)$, normalize capacity by $$\label{eq:rpi:capacity} \mathfrak c(\Sigma,h)=\frac1{(n-2)\omega} \int_E\|dw\|_h^2\,dV_h, \qquad \Delta_h w=0,\quad w\|_\Sigma=1,\quad w\to0.$$
- **mass–capacity deficit** ([source](#stmt-prop-rpi-mass-evolution)): Then $$m(t)\ge c_t\ge0,\qquad \mu(t):=m(t)-c_t\ge0.$$

| q-flow-mass-derivative | m'(t) | = | $-2\mu(t)$ | Almost everywhere in time; the mass is locally absolutely continuous. |
| q-original-coordinate-enclosure-radius | frontier enclosure radius | ≤ | $R_{\max}e^{2t/d}$ | Every flow time; normalized frontiers have a fixed radius bound. |
| q-one-end-small-mass-threshold | m | < | $\delta(n,\eta)R^d$ | Sufficient mass threshold for the stated end-factor deviation bound. |
| q-one-end-factor-deviation | \sup_{r>\ell_nR}\|Z-1\| | < | $\eta$ | Outside the enlarged harmonic coordinate radius. |
| q-harmonic-flat-penrose-bound | m_{\rm ADM}(h) | ≥ | $\frac12(A/\omega)^{d/(n-1)}$ | Full frontier area. |
| q-weighted-singular-tube-volume | \mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}_g\bigl(B_t^g(\mathcal S)\bigr) | ≤ | $C t^8$ | Small tube radii in a buffered compact neighborhood; constants may depend on the minimizer. |

```json
          "exact_quote": "Let $M^n$ carry a smooth metric, let $O$ have smooth boundary, and let $E$ locally minimize perimeter among sets containing $O$. On a relatively compact region meeting $\\partial O$, assume that $\\partial O$ has bounded smooth geometry. Then $E$ has a closed representative whose topological frontier is $T=\\operatorname{supp}|D\\mathbf1_E|_g$. Its regular part has multiplicity one, and its singular set $\\mathcal S$ satisfies $$\\mathcal S=\\varnothing\\quad(n\\le7),\\qquad\n \\dim_{\\mathrm H}\\mathcal S\\le n-8\\quad(n\\ge8).$$ The regular part outside $\\partial O$ is smooth and minimal. Every contact point in $T\\cap\\partial O$ is regular, with local graph in $C^{1,\\alpha}\\cap W^{2,p}$ for every $0<\\alpha<1$ and every $1<p<\\infty$. In dimensions $3\\le n\\le7$ the entire frontier is $C^{1,1}$, with local graph functions in $W^{2,\\infty}$. Moreover, $\\mathcal H^{n-1}_g(\\mathcal S)=0$ and $\\mathcal H^{n-1}_g(T)=P_g(E)$ wherever the total perimeter is finite.",

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-rpi-flow-construction",
          "exact_quote": "Let $K_0$ be the initial outermost minimizing enclosure just described, and put $$E_0=M\\setminus K_0,\\qquad \\Sigma_0=\\partial K_0,\n \\qquad A_0=P_{g_0}(K_0).$$ Here and below perimeter is relative to $\\operatorname{int}M$ and counts the whole frontier. There are increasing closed filled sets $K_t\\supset K_0$, with connected end exteriors $E_t=M\\setminus K_t$, and positive continuous functions $u_t$ such that $$\\label{eq:rpi:flow-integral}\n \\begin{gathered}\n g_t=u_t^{4/d}g_0,\\qquad\n u_t=1+\\int_0^t v_s\\,ds,\\\\\n \\Delta_{g_0}v_t=0\\quad\\hbox{in }E_t,\\qquad\n v_t=0\\quad\\hbox{on }K_t,\\qquad\n v_t\\longrightarrow-e^{-t}\\quad\\hbox{at infinity}.\n \\end{gathered}$$ For every $t$ we have $e^{-t}\\le u_t\\le1$. Each $K_t$ locally minimizes perimeter in the continuous metric $g_t$ against all variations in balls below the initial comparison radius, including variations across $K_0$. The set $K_t$ is the outermost $g_t$-minimizing enclosure of $K_0$ and satisfies $$\\label{eq:rpi:flow-area}\n P_{g_t}(K_t)=A_0.$$ On each finite time interval the conductors have common compact support, the factors have a common positive lower bound and spatial Hölder modulus, and the frontiers have uniform two-phase density and almost-minimizing estimates. In particular there are $\\alpha_T>0$, $C_T<\\infty$, and $r_T>0$ such that $$\\label{eq:rpi:flow-almost-minimizing}\n P_{g_0}(K_t;B_r(x))\n \\le P_{g_0}(L;B_r(x))+C_T r^{k+\\alpha_T}$$ whenever $0\\le t\\le T$, $r<r_T$, and $L\\mathbin{\\triangle}K_t\\Subset B_r(x)$.",

        }
```

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-weighted-singular-control** [claim](#stmt-lem-rpi-bw-quantitative) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rpi-bw-quantitative): Absorb the positive density into a conformal metric, prove strict blow-up compactness, exclude highly symmetric cones at every sufficiently small scale, and apply quantitative stratification. -> Local tube-volume control for compact singular subsets.
- **step-harmonic-positive-mass** [claim](#stmt-lem-rpi-harmonic-pmt) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rpi-harmonic-pmt): Solve a decaying positive Poisson problem, conformally create strictly positive scalar curvature with the required end expansion, apply the independent smooth positive-mass theorem, and remove the perturbation. -> Nonnegative ADM mass at each harmonic end.
- **step-smooth-mass-capacity** [claim](#stmt-lem-rpi-mass-capacity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rpi-mass-capacity): Double the smooth exterior, retain positive conformal factors on both ends, smooth the seam, correct its negative scalar-curvature part, apply positive mass, and then remove the end parameter. -> ADM mass dominates normalized boundary capacity.
- **step-obstacle-regularity** [claim](#stmt-lem-g-obstacle) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-rpi-harmonic-flat-inequality-3): Convert obstacle minimization to almost-minimization, derive density and regularity, prove contact cones are planes, and bootstrap the contact graph equation. -> Regular contact locus and full frontier-area identity, with the stated free singular-set bound.
- **step-full-enclosure-minimum** [claim](#stmt-lem-g-enclosure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-rpi-harmonic-flat-inequality-2): Clip tails using positive coordinate-sphere divergence, use BV compactness, recover full smooth cuts, and fill bounded complementary components. -> An attained positive full enclosure minimum with connected end exterior.
- **step-strict-barrier-detachment** [claim](#stmt-lem-g-detachment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-rpi-harmonic-flat-inequality-4): Adjoin a collar whose normal divergence is strictly negative. Minimality forces collar inclusion; density removes null cracks, and finite-length concatenation proves intrinsic completeness. -> A detached locally minimizing frontier and complete connected closed exterior.
- **step-static-hulls-and-potentials** [claim](#stmt-lem-rpi-static-hull-potential) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rpi-static-hull-potential): Use tail truncation and isoperimetry for confinement, BV compactness and submodularity for the outermost hull, local lattice comparison for obstacle removal, and variational potential construction followed by boundary contraction. -> Static hull existence, uniform density, continuous escape potentials, and Hölder-weight comparison.
- **step-discrete-hull-flow** [claim](#stmt-lem-rpi-discrete-flow) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rpi-discrete-flow): Inductively establish fixed-obstacle global minimality, then apply static hull estimates and integrate bounded harmonic velocities. -> Nested discrete conductors with mesh-independent finite-time estimates and frozen between-grid area.
- **step-discrete-area-loss** [claim](#stmt-prop-rpi-discrete-area) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-rpi-discrete-area): Separate large swept-volume transitions from small ones. Nesting bounds the former, while density and velocity Hölder continuity bound losses in the latter. -> Uniform convergence of discrete frontier areas to the initial area.
- **step-limit-area-before-nesting** [claim](#stmt-lem-rpi-flow-construction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-rpi-discrete-area-2): Extract uniformly convergent conformal factors, identify the limiting minimum perimeter, select outermost hulls, deduce nesting from frontier freezing and the velocity squeeze, and identify integral evolution away from countably many jumps. -> The continuous area-preserving nested conformal flow at every time.
- **step-common-regular-graphs** [claim](#stmt-lem-rpi-simultaneous-regularity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rpi-simultaneous-regularity): Use ordering and strict perimeter compactness to exclude extra sheets near a regular patch, build a dominating one-sided metric extension, and bootstrap harmonic and minimal-graph equations together. -> Common graphical neighborhoods and uniform fixed-order one-sided regularity.
- **step-relative-harmonic-forcing** [claim](#stmt-lem-rpi-relative-harmonic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rpi-relative-harmonic): Normalize positive harmonic functions by corkscrew values, apply boundary Harnack and Schauder estimates, and transfer derivatives using relative Hessian and normal-control errors. -> Relative normal-derivative bounds stable under nearby graph transfer.
- **step-single-median-singular-separation** [claim](#stmt-prop-rpi-singular-separation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-rpi-singular-separation): Identify a common minimizing cone, choose one whole-support distance median and record scales, propagate weak Harnack bounds before controlling forcing, extract a positive limit with vanishing small-ball infimum, and extend a bounded Laplace supersolution across the singular set for the mean-value contradiction. -> Disjoint closed perimeter supports.
- **step-flow-and-candidate-detachment** [claim](#stmt-cor-rpi-flow-detachment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-rpi-flow-detachment): Exclude regular contact by oriented curvature comparison. Verify the common-cone graph equation and relative forcing hypotheses for flow slices and the fixed prescribed-curvature candidate, then apply singular separation. -> Positive distance for every fixed pair of detached compact supports.
- **step-one-sided-smoothing** [claim](#stmt-prop-rpi-one-sided-smoothing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-rpi-one-sided-smoothing): Select regular distance superlevels, prove positive reach, offset the chosen superlevel, transfer accessible-boundary curvature by comparison, and smooth transverse graphs with controlled curvature loss. -> Smooth interior boundaries in shrinking collars with the required curvature sign.
- **step-slice-mass-evolution** [claim](#stmt-prop-rpi-mass-evolution) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-rpi-mass-evolution): Perturb each slice harmonically, enlarge and smooth its conductor, compare capacity with mass, remove the perturbation, and differentiate the normalized end expansion. -> Nonnegative mass–capacity deficit and the almost-everywhere mass evolution identity.
- **step-deficit-and-radius-control** [claim](#stmt-prop-rpi-flow-estimates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-rpi-flow-estimates): Combine deficit integrability with a one-sided slope estimate for decay. Use an escaping-point ball, harmonic factor bounds, and quasiminimizing density to contradict fixed frontier area. -> Vanishing deficit and uniformly bounded normalized frontier radius.
- **step-quantitative-one-end-input** [claim](#stmt-lem-rpi-quantitative-one-end) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rpi-quantitative-one-end): Normalize the harmonic coordinate radius, verify positive mass for the scalar-flat reduction and compact Ricci variations, and apply the independent quantitative argument before restoring scale. -> Small normalized mass implies a uniformly small end-factor deviation.
- **step-complete-auxiliary-approximants** [claim](#stmt-prop-rpi-complete-end-factors) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rpi-quantitative-one-end-2): Confine smooth enclosing conductors to a common tail, double with positive end parameters, repair the seam, compare harmonic monopoles using the capacity deficit, reduce smoothly to one end, and apply quantitative positive mass. -> Complete one-ended approximants and weighted auxiliary-factor convergence on fixed tails.
- **step-harmonic-end-numerical-limit** [claim](#stmt-prop-rpi-harmonic-flat-inequality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-rpi-harmonic-flat-inequality): Integrate the normalized harmonic-factor characteristic equation, transfer the limiting radial potential to the farthest limiting frontier point using uniform boundary estimates, exclude collapse, and compare with enclosing spheres. -> The sharp full-area inequality for a nonempty frontier on a harmonic end.
- **step-smooth-harmonic-density** [claim](#stmt-lem-rpi-smooth-density) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rpi-smooth-density): Cut off the end metric, solve a coercive Dirichlet conformal correction, prove global and compact convergence, and compute annular and boundary fluxes to recover ADM mass. -> Complete harmonic-end approximants preserving nonnegative scalar curvature and converging in mass and metric.
- **step-general-frontier-penrose** [claim](#stmt-thm-rpi) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rpi-smooth-density-2): Use the published smooth-dimensional theorem in its regime. In the singular regime, construct the capacitary perturbation and strict smooth barrier, approximate its exterior, take detached minimizing enclosures, compare all full areas, and pass approximation and perturbation limits in order. Handle empty frontier by density and positive mass. -> The numerical mass–area bound for every exterior in the stated hypothesis class.
- **step-strict-barrier-mass-bound** [claim](#stmt-cor-g-strict-barrier-penrose) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-g-strict-barrier-penrose): Replace the strict smooth inner barrier by its full detached minimizing enclosure and apply the main inequality to the resulting complete connected exterior. -> The mass bound in terms of full smooth enclosing area.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-full-frontier-mass-bound**: ADM mass bounds the full frontier area under the stated exterior, scalar-curvature, asymptotic-decay, and minimizing-frontier hypotheses. Every frontier component contributes to the area.
- **invariant-conformal-flow-area**: The continuous conformal flow preserves the whole frontier perimeter while its canonical filled sets increase and their end exteriors remain connected.
- **invariant-mass-capacity-evolution**: The mass–capacity deficit is nonnegative, and the locally absolutely continuous ADM mass decreases according to the stated evolution identity almost everywhere.
- **invariant-fixed-pair-detachment**: Distinct flow frontiers are disjoint as closed perimeter supports. Each fixed compact pair has positive separation, including at singular points.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-arbitrary-horizon**: Apply the mass–area bound to an arbitrary apparent horizon or stationary boundary. -> Stationarity alone does not supply the local perimeter comparisons or outer minimization required for the bound.
- **anti-use-uncontrolled-end**: Use the theorem on an exterior with additional asymptotic ends or an unverified coordinate decay rate. -> The stated asymptotic regime and mass normalization have not been established.
- **anti-use-negative-scalar-curvature**: Treat the Penrose bound as valid despite negative scalar curvature in the exterior. -> The nonnegative-scalar-curvature input to the conformal and positive-mass comparisons is unavailable.
- **anti-use-reversed-inner-normal**: Apply the smooth mass–capacity inequality with positive mean curvature measured toward the end. -> Reversing the required sign admits the Euclidean exterior example with positive capacity and zero mass.
- **anti-use-uniform-detachment-parameter**: Infer a uniform separation distance while varying the time pair or sending the perturbation parameter to its limit. -> Positive distance for each fixed compact pair does not give a common lower bound across varying pairs or parameters.
- **anti-use-equality-classification**: Conclude Schwarzschild rigidity solely from equality in the numerical inequality. -> The numerical reconstruction does not establish classification of equality or convergence of every frontier component.
- **anti-use-finite-flow-certificate**: Treat agreement on finitely many discrete flow samples as a certificate of the continuous geometric theorem. -> The continuum flow is obtained through compactness, subsequential limits, and identification of limiting minimizing enclosures; sample agreement does not establish these passages.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The result concerns continuous Riemannian exteriors, variational perimeter minimizers, harmonic boundary problems, and limiting conformal flows. The proof explicitly requires: “Choose a mesh sequence tending to zero for which they converge locally uniformly, jointly in space and time, to a positive $u_t$, and put $g_t=u_t^{4/d}g_0$.” This compactness passage and the subsequent identification of limiting enclosures do not provide a finite constructive decision procedure. Finite checks of special metrics would not certify the stated geometric hypotheses or the universal theorem.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The continuous conformal flow is established through compactness and limiting enclosure problems. The paper supplies no finite software contract for representing arbitrary minimizing frontiers or computing the limiting flow. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The enclosure theory concerns complete Riemannian exteriors and full ambient perimeter. It does not construct collision detection, scene boundaries, or discrete simulation geometry. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | One-sided smoothing gives existence of smooth boundaries after choices of distance levels, offsets, and approximation parameters. It does not provide a finite mesh-generation procedure or computable selection thresholds. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The discrete induction requires exact outermost perimeter-minimizing enclosures and exterior harmonic solutions. Its uniform analytic estimates do not supply finite algorithms for those subproblems. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Canonical filled sets and their perimeter supports are continuum objects identified through measure and density properties. The paper specifies no finite representation preserving these properties at singular points. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The relative harmonic estimates require uniformly regular graphical domains and controlled elliptic coefficients. They establish analytic derivative bounds without an accelerator kernel, arithmetic model, or finite workload construction. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The mass–capacity inequality compares geometric ADM mass with normalized Dirichlet energy under curvature and end hypotheses. It supplies no cryptographic primitive, adversarial model, or security construction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The quantitative singular-set estimate follows from local variational minimality in smooth positive weighted geometry. It gives no learning objective, training procedure, or method for certifying these hypotheses from sampled data. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Weighted enclosure minimization has a unique outermost solution in an infinite-dimensional finite-perimeter class. The existence and confinement results do not construct a finite optimisation instance or a solver with certified global optimality. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The vanishing area loss concerns a sequential geometric induction and its limit over a finite time interval. It does not define distributed state updates, communication assumptions, or a consistency protocol. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The numerical Riemannian Penrose inequality is directly relevant to geometric analysis and time-symmetric gravitational initial data. A finite engineering verifier is absent: applying the bound requires independent certification of completeness, scalar curvature, asymptotics, and full frontier minimality. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-complete-connected-exterior** (hypothesis): The main inequality requires a connected complete exterior with its full compact frontier included. Completeness does not assert finite-length accessibility of every singular frontier point. Blocked use: ['Apply the theorem to an incomplete open exterior.', 'Replace the stated completeness convention with accessibility of all singular points.']
- **limit-smooth-ambient-metric** (hypothesis): Frontier singularities are allowed, but the ambient metric must be smooth through the frontier. Blocked use: ['Apply the inequality to a nonsmooth metric across the frontier merely because the frontier itself belongs to the allowed singular class.']
- **limit-scalar-curvature-sign-integrability-decay** (hypothesis): Scalar curvature must be nonnegative and integrable and satisfy the prescribed decay on the end. Blocked use: ['Use the mass bound with negative scalar curvature in the exterior.', 'Discard scalar integrability or end decay when defining the applicable regime.']
- **limit-single-euclidean-end-decay** (hypothesis): The main inequality requires exactly one Euclidean coordinate end outside a compact set and the stated differentiated metric decay. Blocked use: ['Apply the main theorem directly to an exterior with additional ends.', 'Transfer the bound to a different asymptotic geometry or an unverified decay rate.']
- **limit-minimizing-frontier-versus-stationarity** (hypothesis): Both outer minimization and local perimeter minimization are required. Weak stationarity does not supply the local comparison. Blocked use: ['Apply the full-area bound to an arbitrary stationary boundary or apparent horizon.', 'Replace local minimizing comparisons by the minimal-hypersurface equation alone.']
- **limit-full-area-includes-contact-and-components** (formal_scope): The enclosing-area comparison uses full ambient perimeter, including every boundary component and contact with the original obstacle. Blocked use: ['Use relative perimeter that removes obstacle contact area.', 'Apply the full-area conclusion after retaining only a selected frontier component.']
- **limit-obstacle-contact-versus-free-singularities** (edge_case): Smooth obstacle contact is regular, but detachment from the obstacle does not remove possible interior singularities in the higher-dimensional regime. Blocked use: ['Infer smoothness of the entire detached frontier solely from regularity at obstacle contact.', 'Treat a singular free frontier as a smooth boundary for the mass–capacity lemma.']
- **limit-strict-barrier-curvature-sign** (hypothesis): Uniform collar detachment from a smooth inner boundary requires strictly negative mean curvature for the normal pointing into the exterior. Blocked use: ['Infer the same detachment from zero boundary mean curvature.', 'Reverse the normal without reversing the curvature sign.']
- **limit-weighted-hull-quantitative-regime** (hypothesis): Individual weighted-hull existence uses a continuous perimeter weight bounded above and below by positive constants. The uniform density and potential conclusions additionally use initial local minimality and a weight fixed on the initial obstacle; the additive background error requires Hölder control. Blocked use: ['Apply uniform potential estimates to an arbitrary obstacle lacking the initial local comparison.', 'Allow a vanishing perimeter weight.', 'Infer the Hölder-weight error from continuity alone.']
- **limit-discrete-flow-exact-subproblems** (nonconstructive): The discrete induction uses exact outermost minimizing enclosures and exterior harmonic potentials. The source does not turn these continuum subproblems into finite computational procedures. Blocked use: ["Identify a locally optimized discrete hull with the source's outermost global minimizer.", 'Claim a terminating implementation from the discrete induction alone.']
- **limit-area-before-continuum-nesting** (approximation): Vanishing discrete area loss must establish the limiting minimum area before nesting and integral evolution of the limiting outermost hulls are derived. Freezing on discrete conductors alone does not establish freezing on larger limiting conductors. Blocked use: ['Assume limiting nesting in the proof of discrete area preservation.', 'Transfer discrete conductor freezing directly to a potentially larger limiting hull.']
- **limit-continuum-subsequence-and-jump-times** (nonconstructive): The continuum construction uses compactness and subsequence selection. Grid velocities can fail to converge to the selected velocity at jump times, while the integral identity remains valid. Blocked use: ['Claim pointwise convergence of grid velocities at every time.', 'Replace the continuum construction with agreement on finitely many sampled slices.']
- **limit-regular-patch-relative-normalization** (hypothesis): Derivative transfer requires uniformly regular graphical domains and controlled elliptic coefficients. The estimates are relative to the harmonic normalization and do not supply a positive absolute lower bound for that normalization. Blocked use: ['Transfer a fixed-scale Hopf bound without controlling its relative error.', 'Extend regular-patch derivative estimates uniformly through a cone singularity.']
- **limit-singular-separation-full-forcing-contract** (hypothesis): Absence of regular contact is insufficient for singular separation. The ordered supports must satisfy the power comparison, regular-patch graph and metric convergence, elliptic coefficient control, and relative forcing hypotheses. Blocked use: ['Conclude separation from nesting and absence of regular contact alone.', 'Use a positive Jacobi supersolution without the median record-scale infimum argument.']
- **limit-fixed-perturbation-detachment** (hypothesis): Perturbed-enclosure detachment requires a strict harmonic conformal perturbation and a sufficiently small fixed forcing parameter. Positive distance is established for each fixed compact pair, with constants allowed to depend on that pair or parameter. Blocked use: ['Infer separation from a non-strict harmonic factor.', 'Infer a common positive separation distance while varying time pairs or removing the perturbation.']
- **limit-smoothing-accessible-locus-and-inclusion** (hypothesis): One-sided smoothing assumes smooth accessible boundary geometry and a strict outward curvature lower bound. Its application controls conductor inclusion and curvature; boundary-area convergence is not established or needed. Blocked use: ['Apply smoothing to an arbitrary compact boundary without the accessible-locus hypotheses.', 'Infer convergence of boundary areas from shrinking collars.', 'Smooth selected components without establishing separation from the remaining components.']
- **limit-mass-capacity-oriented-sign** (counterexample_scope): The smooth mass–capacity inequality requires nonpositive curvature measured toward the end. The Euclidean exterior has positive curvature with this normal, positive capacity, and zero mass, so the reversed-sign extension fails. Blocked use: ['Apply the smooth mass–capacity bound with positive end-oriented boundary mean curvature.']
- **limit-independent-positive-mass-input** (formal_scope): The proof retains an independent smooth positive-mass theorem. The local weighted-minimizer estimate supplies a singular-set input to that theorem's method and does not replace the theorem itself. Blocked use: ['Describe the reconstruction as a proof of smooth positive mass independent of external inputs.', 'Treat the singular tube estimate as sufficient by itself for positive mass.']
- **limit-positive-doubling-factors-and-completeness** (hypothesis): Positive doubling parameters keep both ends complete during every positive-mass application. Seam smoothing and conformal correction precede removal of the end parameter. Blocked use: ['Set the doubling parameter to its limiting value before applying positive mass.', 'Apply a boundaryless positive-mass theorem directly to the unsmoothed corner metric.']
- **limit-mass-evolution-normalized-almost-everywhere** (formal_scope): The mass evolution law uses normalized coordinates and holds almost everywhere for a locally absolutely continuous mass. The deficit decay proof also uses a one-sided time estimate in addition to integrability. Blocked use: ['Assert a classical mass derivative at every jump time.', 'Infer pointwise deficit decay from integrability alone.', 'Compare masses in unnormalized evolving coordinates as if their asymptotic normalization were fixed.']
- **limit-complete-one-end-quantitative-comparison** (nonconstructive): The quantitative harmonic-factor estimate requires a complete smooth connected boundaryless comparison manifold with a single harmonic end. Its tolerance is existential in the statement, and the auxiliary end factor must first be connected to complete comparison metrics. Blocked use: ['Apply quantitative positive mass directly to an isolated harmonic tail without a complete comparison manifold.', 'Use a universal explicit mass tolerance absent from the source.', 'Skip the complete approximants and smooth one-end reduction.']
- **limit-largest-radius-not-component-convergence** (formal_scope): The numerical conclusion uses the largest limiting frontier radius and enclosing spheres. It does not establish convergence of every frontier component or classify equality. Blocked use: ['Conclude Schwarzschild rigidity solely from equality in the numerical inequality.', 'Infer convergence of each frontier component from the limiting radius argument.']
- **limit-smooth-density-after-fixed-barrier** (approximation): Harmonic-flat approximation is performed on a smooth exterior after a strict smooth barrier has been fixed. Global metric comparison controls all enclosing areas, and the approximation limit precedes removal of the harmonic perturbation. Blocked use: ['Assume a density theorem preserves the original singular minimal frontier.', 'Exchange the approximation and perturbation limits without controlling their dependent constants.', 'Replace full enclosing-area comparison with convergence of one chosen surface.']
- **limit-empty-frontier-positive-mass** (edge_case): Zero full perimeter forces the locally minimizing frontier to be empty. The empty-frontier argument uses harmonic-flat approximation and positive mass rather than the nonempty-frontier flow construction. Blocked use: ['Run the nonempty-frontier limiting-radius proof when the frontier is empty.', 'Permit a nonempty canonical locally minimizing frontier with zero perimeter.']
- **limit-weighted-singular-bound-local-minimization** (hypothesis): The singular tube estimate requires smooth positive weighted geometry and local perimeter-plus-volume minimization. Bounded mean curvature alone is insufficient, and the constants are local to the minimizer and buffered neighborhood. Blocked use: ['Apply the tube estimate to an arbitrary bounded-mean-curvature varifold.', 'Claim constants uniform over unrelated minimizers or metrics.', 'Replace symmetry exclusion at every sufficiently small scale by information about tangent cones alone.']

Put $A_0=P_{g_0}(K_0)$, and let $a$ be continuous with $c\\le a\\le1$ and $a=1$ on $K_0$, where $c>0$. Every $P_a$-minimizing enclosure $K$ has weighted perimeter at most $A_0$ and is contained in a ball $B_{R_*(c)}$ depending only on $c$, $A_0$, the initial support, and the reference geometry.*\n\n*Every such enclosure locally minimizes $P_a$ against unconstrained variations in balls of radius less than $r_0$. Its canonical closed representative has connected open end exterior and uniform two-phase volume and perimeter density bounds. The unique escape potential $q_K$, harmonic in that exterior, zero on $K$, and tending to one at infinity, is continuous and has a Hölder modulus depending on the same data. It satisfies $0<q_K<1$ in the exterior.*\n\n*If in addition $\\operatorname{osc}_{B_r}a\\le C_a r^\\alpha$ for $0<\\alpha<1$, then, for local competitors $L$, $$P_{g_0}(K;B_r)\\le P_{g_0}(L;B_r)+C r^{k+\\alpha},$$ where $C$ also depends on $C_a$.",
```json
          "source_id": "PM-260.10"
        }
      ],
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We give a detailed conformal-flow proof of the numerical Riemannian Penrose inequality in every dimension <i>n</i> ≥ 3 for complete asymptotically flat exteriors with nonnegative scalar curvature and full compact frontiers that are outer minimizing and locally perimeter minimizing. The metric extends smoothly through the possibly singular frontier; neither spin nor frontier connectedness is assumed. The proof follows the conformal-flow approach of Bray, Bray–Lee, and Bi–Zhu.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>

<a id="sec:rpi:introduction"></a>
### The inequality and the proof

The Riemannian Penrose inequality compares the mass of an asymptotically flat manifold with the total area of its outer minimizing minimal boundary. Its geometric content is that a large minimal enclosure requires a correspondingly large mass. Penrose’s physical argument (Penrose 1973) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Penrose1973](#ref-Penrose1973)] related an initial trapped area to the mass remaining after gravitational collapse: cosmic censorship, area increase, and settling to a Kerr black hole led to the proposed mass–area bound. For time-symmetric initial data, the energy condition becomes nonnegative scalar curvature and the horizon condition becomes minimality. The resulting Riemannian problem retains the mass–area comparison without requiring an evolution of the spacetime.

In three dimensions, one route from the horizon to infinity uses the Hawking mass (Hawking 1968) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Hawking1968](#ref-Hawking1968)]. Geroch’s calculation (Geroch 1973) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Geroch1973](#ref-Geroch1973)] showed its monotonicity along smooth inverse mean curvature flow of two-spheres in nonnegative scalar curvature. Jang and Wald (Jang and Wald 1977, sec. 5) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[JangWald1977](#ref-JangWald1977)] derived the Penrose bound assuming a global flow with the required asymptotics. Existence of such a flow was the central difficulty: mean curvature may vanish and the smooth evolution can develop singularities. Before the general proofs, spherical symmetry yielded the inequality (Malec and Ó Murchadha 1994) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[MalecOMurchadha1994](#ref-MalecOMurchadha1994)], and spinorial methods gave mass–area estimates involving an additional normalized Sobolev quantity (Herzlich 1997) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Herzlich1997](#ref-Herzlich1997)].

Huisken and Ilmanen’s weak inverse mean curvature flow allows jumps across the regions where smooth flow fails (Huisken and Ilmanen 2001) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[HuiskenIlmanen2001](#ref-HuiskenIlmanen2001)]. It proves the sharp mass bound for each connected component of an outermost minimal boundary in dimension three. Bray’s conformal flow (Bray 2001) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Bray2001](#ref-Bray2001)] instead changes the ambient metric while preserving the total horizon area. Positive mass makes the evolving mass nonincreasing, and the limiting comparison yields the sharp bound for the full area, including disconnected boundaries. The harmonic-flat reduction, area-preserving flow, and mass comparison in the present proof descend from this construction.

Bray and Lee extended the conformal method to dimensions below eight (Bray and Lee 2009) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[BrayLee2009](#ref-BrayLee2009)]. The dimension restriction reflects the use of smooth minimizing hypersurfaces. In dimensions at least eight, a minimizing frontier may be singular, and its singular points affect the flow construction, separation of slices, and the geometric comparison used to decrease mass.

Restricted all-dimensional results were obtained earlier under graphical hypotheses. Lam (Lam 2010, Corollary 7) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Lam2010Graphs](#ref-Lam2010Graphs)] proved the inequality for asymptotically flat Euclidean graphs with nonnegative scalar curvature and convex horizon components. De Lima and Girão (Lima and Girão 2015, Theorem 1.8) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[deLimaGirao2015](#ref-deLimaGirao2015)] treated two-sided asymptotically flat quasi-graphs in Euclidean space, with nonnegative scalar curvature and convex horizons in a horizontal hyperplane met orthogonally. These results use additional graphical control of the geometry.

Bi and Zhu (Bi and Zhu 2026b, Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[BiZhu2026v2](#ref-BiZhu2026v2)] prove the all-dimensional inequality together with Schwarzschild rigidity for almost-minimizing Caccioppoli (finite-perimeter) boundaries whose regular part is minimal and which are their own outermost minimizing enclosures, with a $C^{2,\alpha}$ metric extension through the boundary. Their version 2 conformal-flow argument is the immediate framework for this paper. We develop the numerical proof under stronger local minimization and ambient regularity hypotheses: the frontier is locally perimeter minimizing on both sides, the ambient metric is smooth through it, and the scalar curvature has the pointwise decay stated below. Our initially outer minimizing frontier is replaced by its outermost hull without changing its area. The present contribution is a detailed numerical reconstruction for this minimizing-frontier class.

The reconstruction makes explicit the preservation of area before limiting nesting, uniform estimates near regular patches of tangent cones, and complete smooth comparison metrics with positive conformal factors. It uses only the largest limiting frontier radius to reach the numerical conclusion. These reductions explain which geometric and positive-mass inputs suffice without requiring convergence of every frontier component or classification of equality.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Manifolds, frontiers, and mass

Write $n\ge3$, $k=n-1$, $d=n-2$, and $\omega=\mathcal H^k_\delta(S^k)$. We use $O_j(r^{-\gamma})$ for a tensor whose coordinate derivatives of order $\ell\le j$ are $O(r^{-\gamma-\ell})$. On a Euclidean coordinate end the ADM mass is $$m_{\rm ADM}(h)=\frac1{2k\omega}
 \lim_{R\to\infty}\int_{S_R}
 (\partial_jh_{ij}-\partial_ih_{jj})n_\delta^i\,dA_\delta.$$ The decay and scalar integrability hypotheses below give the usual well-defined mass in this normalization; see (Bartnik 1986) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Bartnik1986](#ref-Bartnik1986)].

A possibly singular exterior is described by its filled side in a smooth ambient manifold. The exterior contains the coordinate end; its compact frontier $\Sigma$ is the support of the perimeter measure of the filled side.
